$\mathcal D_\Sigma$. Thus $\delta(\theta)$ is independent of
the choices of $P_U$ and $P_p$.
An isomorphism $E_\theta\simeq E'_\theta$ inducing the identity
on $\mathbb F_2$ and $G$ transports the actions on $P_U$ and every $P_p$.
The induced $G$-actions on $D_p$ remain unchanged, so the classes
$\chi_p\in Q_p$ do not depend on the representative extension.
For $\theta_1,\theta_2\in\mathscr L$ and
$V\in\{U\}\cup\{B_p:p\in\Sigma\}$, choose central lifts
$P_{1,V},P_{2,V}\to\widetilde S_V$ and put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-baer-lifts}
 P_{12,V}=(P_{1,V}\times_{\widetilde S_V}P_{2,V})/\Delta\mathbb F_2,
 \qquad E_{12}=(E_{\theta_1}\times_G E_{\theta_2})/\Delta\mathbb F_2.
\end{equation}
Proposition~\ref{prop:realizable-subspace} gives an $E_{12}$-action
on $P_{12,V}$ and identifies the class of $E_{12}$ with
$\theta_1+\theta_2$. Let $\chi_{i,p}$ be the class defined by
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-delta-definition} using $P_{i,U},P_{i,B_p}$.
The difference between $(P_{12,U})_{K_p}$ and
$(P_{12,B_p})_{K_p}$ is the contracted product of the torsors
representing $\chi_{1,p}$ and $\chi_{2,p}$. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-delta-additivity}
 \delta(\theta_1+\theta_2)
 =[(\chi_{1,p}+\chi_{2,p})_{p\in\Sigma}]
 =\delta(\theta_1)+\delta(\theta_2).
\end{equation}

If $P_{\mathbb Z}\to\widetilde S$ is a central lift of $\theta$, take
$P_U=(P_{\mathbb Z})_U$ and $P_p=(P_{\mathbb Z})_{B_p}$. Then
$(P_U)_{K_p}=(P_p)_{K_p}$, so $\chi_p=0$ for every $p\in\Sigma$.
Thus $\delta(\theta)=0$.  Conversely, suppose that
$\delta(\theta)=0$.  Then there are
$\eta\in H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(X,\mathbb F_2)$ and $\eta_p\in\mathcal B_p^1$
with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-compatible-twists}
 \chi_p=\eta|_{C_{K_p}}+\operatorname{res}_p^1(\eta_p)
 \quad\text{in }Q_p\quad(p\in\Sigma).
\end{equation}  Twist $P_U$ by the pullback to
$\widetilde S_U$ of a torsor representing $\eta$, and twist $P_p$
by the pullback along $r_{B_p}$ of an $A$-equivariant torsor
representing $\eta_p$.  Keep the notation $P_U,P_p$ for the twisted torsors. By
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-choice-change} and
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-compatible-twists}, the difference between
$(P_U)_{K_p}$ and $(P_p)_{K_p}$ now represents $0\in Q_p$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:local-lift-criterion} therefore gives
$E_\theta$-equivariant isomorphisms
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-generic-lift-isomorphisms}
 (P_U)_{K_p}\simeq(P_p)_{K_p}
       \quad\text{over }\widetilde C_{K_p}.
\end{equation}
For $\theta=0$, the identity cover of $\widetilde S$ realizes
$\theta$ by definition. Suppose that $\theta\ne0$; then $P_U$
is connected. Let $M=\mathbb Q(P_U)$, and let
$\mathcal N\to\widetilde S$ be the normalization of
$\widetilde S$ in $M/\mathbb Q(\widetilde C)$. Excellence of
$\widetilde S$ makes $\mathcal N\to\widetilde S$ finite.
The scheme $P_U$ is normal, finite over $\widetilde S_U$, and has
function field $M$. Uniqueness of normalization gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-global-normalization-restriction}
 \mathcal N_U\simeq P_U\quad\text{over }\widetilde S_U.
\end{equation}

Put $L_p=K_p(\widetilde C_{K_p})$.  Normalization commutes with
\'etale localization and henselization (\href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/03GE}{\textup{[Stacks, 03GE]}}, \href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0CBF}{\textup{[Stacks, 0CBF]}}), so
$\mathcal N_{B_p}$ is the integral closure of $\widetilde S_{B_p}$ in
$M\otimes_{\mathbb Q(\widetilde C)}L_p$.  The isomorphism
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-generic-lift-isomorphisms} identifies
$M\otimes_{\mathbb Q(\widetilde C)}L_p$ with the algebra of the generic
fibre of $P_p\to\widetilde S_{B_p}$. Since $P_p$ is normal and
finite over $\widetilde S_{B_p}$, uniqueness of integral closure
gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-henselian-normalization-restriction}
 \mathcal N_{B_p}\simeq P_p\quad\text{over }\widetilde S_{B_p}.
\end{equation}
Consequently $\mathcal N_{B_p}\to\widetilde S_{B_p}$ is \'etale.  Faithfully flat descent along
$\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z_{(p)}^h\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z_{(p)}$ then shows that
$\mathcal N\to\widetilde S$ is \'etale at the points over $p$.  Equation~\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-global-normalization-restriction}
also proves that $\mathcal N_U\to\widetilde S_U$ is \'etale.
Since every point of $\widetilde S$ lies over $U$ or over a prime
in $\Sigma$, the morphism $\mathcal N\to\widetilde S$ is
\'etale. For an affine open $V=\operatorname{Spec}A_V\subseteq S_{-1}$,
write $(q\circ r)^{-1}(V)=\operatorname{Spec}B_V$ and let $D_V$
be the integral closure of $B_V$ in $M$. The group $E_\theta$
fixes $A_V$ pointwise and preserves $\mathbb Q(\widetilde C)$.
Since $B_V$ is the integral closure of $A_V$ in
$\mathbb Q(\widetilde C)$, every $e\in E_\theta$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-equivariant-normalization-rings}
 e(B_V)=B_V,\qquad e(D_V)=D_V.
\end{equation}
The actions on $\operatorname{Spec}D_V$ agree after restriction to
$V\cap V'$ for any affine opens $V,V'\subseteq S_{-1}$, because
all actions are induced by the action on $M$. Hence the actions on
$\operatorname{Spec}D_V$ define an $E_\theta$-action on $\mathcal N$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:realizing-surface-properties} proves that
$\mathcal N$ is regular, integral, projective and flat over
$\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z$, that $\mathcal N_{\mathbb Q}$ is
smooth and geometrically integral, and that
$\mathcal N\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z$ has a section.
Thus $\theta\in\mathscr E(\widetilde S,S_{-1})$, proving
$\ker\delta=\mathscr E(\widetilde S,S_{-1})$.

Finally, the definition of $\mathcal O$ and the inclusions in
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-obstruction-filtration} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-obstruction-dimension-sum}
 \begin{aligned}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal O(\widetilde S,S_{-1})
 &=\dim_{\mathbb F_2}(H^2(G,\mathbb F_2)/\mathscr R)
   +\dim_{\mathbb F_2}(\mathscr R/\mathscr L)
   +\dim_{\mathbb F_2}(\mathscr L/\mathscr E)\\
 &\le\sum_{p\in\Sigma}
   \bigl(\dim_{\mathbb F_2}\operatorname{im}\rho_p+\dim_{\mathbb F_2}\ker\rho_p\bigr)
     +\dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal D_\Sigma\\
 &=\sum_{p\in\Sigma}\dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal B_p^2
     +\dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal D_\Sigma.
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The inequality uses \eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-rational-obstruction-injection},
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-local-integral-injection} and
$\ker\delta=\mathscr E$. This proves
\eqref{eq:bad-only-obstruction-budget}.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- proposition-22: body_md -->
\label{prop:realizable-local-bounds}
For an anchored diagram, with $t=|\Sigma|-1$, one has for every
$p\in\Sigma$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-local-dimension-bounds}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal B_p^2\le
 \begin{cases}
 324g+324,&p\ne2,\\[2pt]
 (3g+1)/2,&p=2,
 \end{cases}
 \qquad
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal D_\Sigma\le(2g+2)t+(2g+3).
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-22 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Fix $p\in\Sigma$, and put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-geometric-fibres}
 T=(S_0\times_{\mathbb Z}\overline{\mathbb F}_p)_{\mathrm{red}},
 \qquad
 Y=(S_{-1}\times_{\mathbb Z}\overline{\mathbb F}_p)_{\mathrm{red}}.
\end{equation}
Let $q_p:T\to Y$ be the finite morphism induced by $q:S_0\to S_{-1}$.
On the \'etale site of $Y$, define the group-cochain complex
$\mathcal K^\bullet$ and its cohomology sheaves $\mathcal H^b$ by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-stabilizer-cochain-sheaf}
 \mathcal K^\bullet=C^\bullet(A,q_{p*}\mathbb F_2),\qquad
 \mathcal K^n=\prod_{A^n}q_{p*}\mathbb F_2,\qquad
 \mathcal H^b=H^b(\mathcal K^\bullet).
\end{equation}
The direct image functor $q_{p*}$ is exact on \'etale sheaves.
Indeed, for a geometric point $\bar y$ of $Y$, there is a decomposition
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-strict-local-finite-decomposition}
 T\times_Y\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal O^{\mathrm{sh}}_{Y,\bar y}=\coprod_j T_j,
\end{equation}
where each $T_j$ is the spectrum of a strictly henselian local ring.  For every abelian
\'etale sheaf $\mathcal G$ on $T_j$,
$H^i_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(T_j,\mathcal G)=0$ for $i>0$; see \href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/03QP}{\textup{[Stacks, 03QP]}},
\href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/03QO}{\textup{[Stacks, 03QO]}}.  The stalk formula therefore gives
$(R^iq_{p*}\mathcal G)_{\bar y}=0$ for every $i>0$, every abelian
\'etale sheaf $\mathcal G$ on $T$, and every geometric point
$\bar y$ of $Y$. Hence $R^iq_{p*}=0$ for $i>0$.
The hypercohomology spectral sequence of $\mathcal K^\bullet$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:realizable-stabilizer-sequence}
 H^a_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal H^b)\ \Longrightarrow\ H_A^{a+b}(T,\mathbb F_2),
 \qquad
 (\mathcal H^b)_{\bar y}=H^b(I_{\bar y},\mathbb F_2),\qquad
 \mathcal H^0=\mathbb F_2.
\end{equation}
Here $I_{\bar y}=\{a\in A:a\bar x=\bar x\}$ for a chosen
geometric point $\bar x\in T$ over $\bar y\in Y$.
For the stalk formula in \eqref{eq:realizable-stabilizer-sequence},
we first prove that $A$ acts transitively on the geometric points of
$q_p^{-1}(\bar y)$. Choose an affine neighbourhood
$\operatorname{Spec} R\subseteq S_{-1}$ of the image of $\bar y$.
Write $q^{-1}(\operatorname{Spec} R)=\operatorname{Spec} B$.  Since $S_0$ is the normalization
of $S_{-1}$ in $\mathbb Q((S_0)_{\mathbb Q})$, $B$ is the integral closure of
$R$ in that field and $B^A=R$.  For
$R^{\mathrm{sh}}=\mathcal O^{\mathrm{sh}}_{S_{-1},\bar y}$, flat base change gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-strict-local-invariants}
 (B\otimes_R R^{\mathrm{sh}})^A=R^{\mathrm{sh}}.
\end{equation}
Indeed, invariants are the kernel of the finite collection of maps
$b\mapsto a(b)-b$, for $a\in A$, and this kernel commutes with flat
base change.  The finite $R^{\mathrm{sh}}$-algebra
$B\otimes_R R^{\mathrm{sh}}$ is a product of local rings, one for each
geometric point over $\bar y$.  Write $B\otimes_RR^{\mathrm{sh}}=\prod_{j\in J}B_j$ with $B_j$ local,
and let $e_j$ be the idempotent equal to $1$ in $B_j$ and $0$ in
every $B_k$ for $k\ne j$. If an $A$-orbit $J'\subseteq J$
satisfied $\varnothing\ne J'\ne J$, then
$e=\sum_{j\in J'}e_j$ would satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-invariant-idempotent}
 e^2=e,\qquad e\notin\{0,1\},\qquad
 e\in(B\otimes_RR^{\mathrm{sh}})^A=R^{\mathrm{sh}}.
\end{equation}
A local ring has only the idempotents $0$ and $1$, contradicting
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-invariant-idempotent}. Hence $J$ is a
single $A$-orbit. This proves transitivity on $q_p^{-1}(\bar y)$,
also when $p=2$. It follows that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-shapiro-stalk}
 (q_{p*}\mathbb F_2)_{\bar y}\simeq\mathbb F_2[A/I_{\bar y}],\qquad
 (\mathcal H^b)_{\bar y}
 \simeq H^b(A,\mathbb F_2[A/I_{\bar y}])
 \simeq H^b(I_{\bar y},\mathbb F_2),
\end{equation}
where the last isomorphism is Shapiro's lemma.
Passing from $S_0\times_{\mathbb Z}\overline{\mathbb F}_p$ to $T$
and from $S_{-1}\times_{\mathbb Z}\overline{\mathbb F}_p$ to $Y$
removes nilpotents. The \'etale sites are unchanged by either
reduction (\href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/03SI}{\textup{[Stacks, 03SI]}}).

Suppose first that $p$ is odd.  Write $Y_1,\ldots,Y_c$ for the
irreducible components of $Y$, $Z=\operatorname{Sing}(Y)$ for the set of nodes of $Y$,
and $Y_i^\circ=Y_i\setminus Z$.  Put $s=|Z|=s_p$ and $a=a_p$, so $c=c_p$.
For a geometric point $\bar y\in Y$, put
$R_{\bar y}=\mathcal O^{\mathrm{sh}}_{S_{-1},\bar y}$ and
$F_{\bar y}=\operatorname{Frac}(R_{\bar y})$.
The ring $R_{\bar y}$ is regular and factorial. Since $p\ne2$,
Hensel's lemma gives $R_{\bar y}^{\times}=R_{\bar y}^{\times2}$.
For $\chi\in\operatorname{Hom}(A,\mathbb F_2)\setminus\{0\}$, choose
$a_\chi\in\mathbb Q(C)^\times$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-character-subfields}
 \mathbb Q((S_0)_{\mathbb Q})^{\ker\chi}=\mathbb Q(C)(\sqrt{a_\chi}),
\end{equation}
and put $a_0=1$. Since $q_{\mathbb Q}$ is \'etale, the valuation of
$a_\chi$ along every horizontal prime divisor of $S_{-1}$ is even.
If $\bar y\in Y_i^\circ$, choose a local equation $u=0$ for the
component of the fibre through $\bar y$. If $\bar y\in Z$, choose
local equations $u=0$ and $v=0$ for the two components through
$\bar y$. Unique factorization in $R_{\bar y}$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-local-character-square-classes}
 [a_\chi]\in
 \begin{cases}
   \langle[u]\rangle\subseteq F_{\bar y}^{\times}/F_{\bar y}^{\times2},
          &\bar y\in Y_i^\circ,\\
   \langle[u],[v]\rangle\subseteq F_{\bar y}^{\times}/F_{\bar y}^{\times2},
          &\bar y\in Z.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-odd-stabilizer-ranks}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}I_{\bar y}\le1\quad(\bar y\in Y_i^\circ),\qquad
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}I_{\bar y}\le2\quad(\bar y\in Z).
\end{equation}
Let $B_p^{\mathrm{sh}}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb Z_{(p)}^{\mathrm{sh}})$,
where the strict henselization is taken with residue field
$\overline{\mathbb F}_p$, and put $\mathscr Y=(S_{-1})_{B_p^{\mathrm{sh}}}$.
Let $\eta_i$ be the generic point of $Y_i\subseteq\mathscr Y$,
and let $v_i$ be the normalized discrete valuation of
$\mathcal O_{\mathscr Y,\eta_i}$. Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-component-stabilizer}
 V_i=\{\chi\in\operatorname{Hom}(A,\mathbb F_2):v_i(a_\chi)\in2\mathbb Z\},
 \qquad I_i=\bigcap_{\chi\in V_i}\ker\chi\subseteq A.
\end{equation}
For every $\bar y\in Y_i^\circ$, equation
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-local-character-square-classes} identifies
$I_{\bar y}$ with $I_i$. More precisely, over $R_{\bar y}$ the
normalization has no ramified factor other than the quadratic algebra
$R_{\bar y}[t]/(t^2-u)$. The reduction of this algebra modulo $u$
is $(R_{\bar y}/(u))[t]/(t^2)$, whose reduced ring is
$R_{\bar y}/(u)$. It follows that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-component-residual-torsor}
 (q_p^{-1}(Y_i^\circ))_{\mathrm{red}}\longrightarrow Y_i^\circ
 \quad\text{is an \'etale }A/I_i\text{-torsor}.
\end{equation}
Pulling back $\mathcal H^b|_{Y_i^\circ}$ along
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-component-residual-torsor} gives the constant
sheaf with fibre $H^b(I_i,\mathbb F_2)$. The descent automorphism associated
with $a\in A$ is induced by $h\mapsto aha^{-1}$ on $I_i$.
Since $A$ is abelian, $aha^{-1}=h$, so every descent automorphism
is the identity. Thus
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-constant-stabilizer-cohomology}
 \mathcal H^b|_{Y_i^\circ}\simeq
       \underline{H^b(I_i,\mathbb F_2)}_{Y_i^\circ}.
\end{equation}

For $I\simeq\mathbb F_2^r$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-elementary-abelian-cohomology}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^1(I,\mathbb F_2)=r,\qquad
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^2(I,\mathbb F_2)=r(r+1)/2,
\end{equation}
as one sees by tensoring the periodic resolutions of $C_2$.
Equations~\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-shapiro-stalk},
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-odd-stabilizer-ranks} and
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-elementary-abelian-cohomology} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-stalk-dimension-bounds}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}(\mathcal H^1)_{\bar y}\le2,\qquad
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}(\mathcal H^2)_{\bar y}\le3\qquad(\bar y\in Y).
\end{equation}  Let
$\nu:\widetilde Y=\coprod_{i=1}^c\widetilde Y_i\to Y$ be the
normalization, let $a_i$ be the genus of $\widetilde Y_i$, and let
$i_z:\{z\}\to Y$ be the inclusion of a node.  Let ${\mathbb F_2}_Y$ and ${\mathbb F_2}_{\widetilde Y}$ be the constant sheaves
on $Y$ and $\widetilde Y$, respectively. There is an exact sequence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-normalization-sequence}
 0\longrightarrow{\mathbb F_2}_Y\longrightarrow\nu_*{\mathbb F_2}_{\widetilde Y}
 \longrightarrow\bigoplus_{z\in Z}i_{z*}\mathbb F_2\longrightarrow0
\end{equation}
in which the component at $z$ of the last map sends a pair of
values $(a,b)\in\mathbb F_2\oplus\mathbb F_2$ at the two points of $\nu^{-1}(z)$
to $a-b$. The cohomology groups of the smooth projective curve
$\widetilde Y_i$ satisfy
$\dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(\widetilde Y_i,\mathbb F_2)=2a_i$ and
$\dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^2_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(\widetilde Y_i,\mathbb F_2)=1$.
Since a sheaf supported on the finite set $Z$ has zero cohomology in
positive degree, the long exact sequence of
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-normalization-sequence} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-nodal-cohomology-bounds}
 h^1(Y)\le2a+s,\qquad h^2(Y)=c.
\end{equation}

More generally, let $\mathcal F$ be a constructible $\mathbb F_2$-sheaf on
$Y$, of stalk rank at most $b$, whose restriction to each
$Y_i^\circ$ is constant.  Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-constructible-cohomology-bounds}
 \dim H^0_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal F)\le b(c+s),\qquad
 \dim H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal F)\le b(2a+2s+2c).
\end{equation}
Choose a geometric point $\bar\eta_i\in Y_i^\circ$ for each $i$.
Restriction defines an injection
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-constructible-section-injection}
 H^0_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal F)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow
 \bigoplus_{i=1}^{c}\mathcal F_{\bar\eta_i}
 \oplus\bigoplus_{z\in Z}\mathcal F_z.
\end{equation}
Indeed, $\mathcal F|_{Y_i^\circ}$ is constant and $Y_i^\circ$ is
connected, so a section vanishing at $\bar\eta_i$ vanishes on
$Y_i^\circ$. A section with zero image in
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-constructible-section-injection} therefore
vanishes at every geometric point of $Y$ and is zero. Each summand
on the right has dimension at most $b$, proving
$\dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^0_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal F)\le b(c+s)$.
To bound $\dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal F)$, put
$Y^\circ=Y\setminus Z$, and write
$j:Y^\circ\hookrightarrow Y$ and $i:Z\hookrightarrow Y$.
The exact sequence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-constructible-localization}
 0\longrightarrow j_!j^*\mathcal F\longrightarrow\mathcal F
 \longrightarrow i_*i^*\mathcal F\longrightarrow0
\end{equation}
and $H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Z,i^*\mathcal F)=0$ give a surjection
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-compact-support-surjection}
 H_c^1(Y^\circ,j^*\mathcal F)
 \twoheadrightarrow H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal F).
\end{equation}  Put
$d_i=|\nu^{-1}(Z)\cap\widetilde Y_i|$, so
$Y_i^\circ\simeq\widetilde Y_i\setminus\nu^{-1}(Z)$.
The compactly supported cohomology of $Y_i^\circ$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-punctured-component-cohomology}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}H_c^1(Y_i^\circ,\mathbb F_2)=
 \begin{cases}2a_i+d_i-1,&d_i>0,\\2a_i,&d_i=0.\end{cases}
\end{equation}
Let $b_i=\dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal F_{\bar\eta_i}\le b$.
Since $\sum_i d_i=2s$ and $\sum_i a_i=a$, equations
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-compact-support-surjection} and
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-punctured-component-cohomology} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-constructible-first-dimension}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal F)
 \le\sum_{i=1}^c b_i\dim_{\mathbb F_2}H_c^1(Y_i^\circ,\mathbb F_2)
 \le b\sum_{i=1}^c(2a_i+d_i)=b(2a+2s).
\end{equation}
This implies the stated bound
$\dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal F)\le b(2a+2s+2c)$ in
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-constructible-cohomology-bounds}.
For the cohomology formulas used in
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-nodal-cohomology-bounds} and
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-punctured-component-cohomology}, and the
localization sequence \eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-constructible-localization},
see \href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/03RQ}{\textup{[Stacks, 03RQ]}}, \href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/03S2}{\textup{[Stacks, 03S2]}}.

The filtration on $H_A^n(T,\mathbb F_2)$ obtained from
\eqref{eq:realizable-stabilizer-sequence} has associated graded
pieces that are subquotients of
$H^a_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal H^b)$ for $a+b=n$. For $n=1,2$,
apply \eqref{eq:obs-explicit-nodal-cohomology-bounds},
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-constructible-cohomology-bounds} and
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-stalk-dimension-bounds}. We obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-odd-geometric-equivariant-bounds}
 \begin{aligned}
 h_A^1(T)&\le h^1(Y)+\dim H^0_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal H^1)
              \le2a+2c+3s,\\
 h_A^2(T)&\le h^2(Y)+\dim H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal H^1)
                    +\dim H^0_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal H^2)
              \le4a+8c+7s.
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
For $p=2$, also put
$B_2^{\mathrm{sh}}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb Z_{(2)}^{\mathrm{sh}})$, with
residue field $\overline{\mathbb F}_2$.  Proper base change (\href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/095T}{\textup{[Stacks, 095T]}}) gives $A$-equivariant
isomorphisms on ordinary cohomology and therefore gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-equivariant-proper-base-change}
 H_A^b((S_0)_{B_p^{\mathrm{sh}}},\mathbb F_2)\simeq H_A^b(T,\mathbb F_2).
\end{equation}
Write $\Phi_p=\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb F}_p/\mathbb F_p)$.
Descent along $B_p^{\mathrm{sh}}\to B_p$ gives the spectral sequence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-henselian-descent-sequence}
 H^a\bigl(\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb F}_p/\mathbb F_p),
                 H_A^b(T,\mathbb F_2)\bigr)
 \ \Longrightarrow\ H_A^{a+b}((S_0)_{B_p},\mathbb F_2).
\end{equation}
The group $\Phi_p$ has torsion cohomological dimension one. Thus
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-henselian-descent-sequence} gives an exact sequence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-henselian-degree-two-exact}
 0\longrightarrow H^1(\Phi_p,H_A^1(T,\mathbb F_2))
 \longrightarrow\mathcal B_p^2
 \longrightarrow H^0(\Phi_p,H_A^2(T,\mathbb F_2))\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation}
For a finite-dimensional $\mathbb F_2$-vector space $W$ with a continuous
$\Phi_p$-action, let $\varphi_p$ be a topological generator of
$\Phi_p$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-finite-field-cohomology}
 H^0(\Phi_p,W)=\ker(\varphi_p-1:W\to W),\qquad
 H^1(\Phi_p,W)=\operatorname{coker}(\varphi_p-1:W\to W).
\end{equation}
The kernel and cokernel in
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-finite-field-cohomology} have dimension at
most $\dim_{\mathbb F_2}W$. Equation
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-henselian-degree-two-exact} therefore gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:realizable-trait-bound}
 \dim\mathcal B_p^2\le h_A^2(T)+h_A^1(T).
\end{equation}
For odd $p$, equations \eqref{eq:realizable-trait-bound},
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-odd-geometric-equivariant-bounds} and
\eqref{eq:real-explicit-fibre-bounds} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-odd-trait-bound}
 \dim\mathcal B_p^2\le6a_p+10c_p+10s_p
       \le162N=324g+324.
\end{equation}

For $p=2$, let $\nu:\widetilde Y\to Y=Y_2$ be the normalization
and let $Z=\operatorname{Sing}(Y)$.  For every $z\in Z$, the set $\nu^{-1}(z)$ has two points.
Consequently the cokernel of
${\mathbb F_2}_Y\to\nu_*{\mathbb F_2}_{\widetilde Y}$ has stalk $\mathbb F_2$ at $z$
and zero stalk at each point of $Y\setminus Z$, so
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-normalization-sequence} also holds for $p=2$.
Write $\widetilde Y=\coprod_{i=1}^c\widetilde Y_i$. By the
hypotheses on an anchored diagram,
$\widetilde Y_i\simeq\mathbb P^1_{\overline{\mathbb F}_2}$.
Since $Y$ is connected and
$H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(\mathbb P^1_{\overline{\mathbb F}_2},\mathbb F_2)=0$, the
long exact sequence of \eqref{eq:obs-explicit-normalization-sequence}
reduces in degrees zero and one to
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-dyadic-normalization-cohomology}
 0\longrightarrow\mathbb F_2\longrightarrow\mathbb F_2^c
 \xrightarrow{\partial}\mathbb F_2^{|Z|}
 \longrightarrow H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathbb F_2)\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation}
The coordinate of $\partial(a_1,\ldots,a_c)$ at $z$ is the
difference of the values on the two components containing the points
of $\nu^{-1}(z)$. Thus $\partial$ is the incidence map of
$\Gamma_Y=\Gamma(Y)$ from
\eqref{eq:bg-explicit-incidence-graph}, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-dyadic-incidence-bound}
 h^1(Y)=b_1(\Gamma_Y)\le(g-1)/2,
\end{equation}
by the assumed bound on $b_1(\Gamma_Y)$.

To compute $H^2_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathbb F_2)$, use the Artin--Schreier sequence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-artin-schreier-sequence}
 0\longrightarrow\mathbb F_2\longrightarrow\mathcal O_Y
 \xrightarrow{F-1}\mathcal O_Y\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad F(f)=f^2.
\end{equation}
Since $Y$ is a proper curve, $H^i(Y,\mathcal O_Y)=0$ for $i>1$,
and $H^1(Y,\mathcal O_Y)$ is finite-dimensional over
$\overline{\mathbb F}_2$. The induced map $F$ on
$H^1(Y,\mathcal O_Y)$ satisfies $F(av)=a^2F(v)$. For an
endomorphism with this semilinearity property, the map $F-1$ is
surjective; see \href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0A3J}{\textup{[Stacks, 0A3J]}}, \href{https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0A3L}{\textup{[Stacks, 0A3L]}}. The long exact sequence of
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-artin-schreier-sequence} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-dyadic-degree-two-vanishing}
 H^2_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathbb F_2)
 =\operatorname{coker}\bigl(F-1:H^1(Y,\mathcal O_Y)
                                  \to H^1(Y,\mathcal O_Y)\bigr)=0.
\end{equation}
By the hypotheses on the stabilizers, there is a finite subset
$Z_A\subseteq Y$ with $|Z_A|\le b=(g+1)/2$ such that
$I_{\bar y}=\{1\}$ for $\bar y\notin Z_A$ and
$|I_{\bar y}|\le2$ for $\bar y\in Z_A$.
Equations~\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-shapiro-stalk} and
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-elementary-abelian-cohomology} therefore give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-dyadic-supported-sheaf-bounds}
 \begin{gathered}
 \operatorname{Supp}(\mathcal H^j)\subseteq Z_A,\qquad
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}(\mathcal H^j)_{\bar y}\le1\quad(j=1,2),\\
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^0_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal H^j)\le b,\qquad
 H^i_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(Y,\mathcal H^j)=0\quad(i>0,\ j=1,2).
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Apply \eqref{eq:realizable-stabilizer-sequence}, using
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-dyadic-incidence-bound},
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-dyadic-degree-two-vanishing} and
\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-dyadic-supported-sheaf-bounds}. We obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-dyadic-equivariant-bounds}
 h_A^1(T)\le(g-1)/2+b,\qquad h_A^2(T)\le b.
\end{equation}
Equations~\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-dyadic-equivariant-bounds} and
\eqref{eq:realizable-trait-bound} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-dyadic-trait-dimension}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal B_2^2
 \le b+\frac{g-1}{2}+b
 =\frac{3g+1}{2}.
\end{equation}

Finally, the degree-one Hochschild--Serre sequence for
$C_{K_p}\to\operatorname{Spec} K_p$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-local-hochschild-serre}
 0\longrightarrow H^1(K_p,\mathbb F_2)\longrightarrow Q_p
 \longrightarrow H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(C_{\overline K_p},\mathbb F_2)^{G_{K_p}},
\end{equation}
where $G_{K_p}=\operatorname{Gal}(\overline K_p/K_p)$.  Since $C$ has genus $g$, we have
$\dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(C_{\overline K_p},\mathbb F_2)=2g$ and therefore
$\dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(C_{\overline K_p},\mathbb F_2)^{G_{K_p}}\le2g$.  Hence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-local-twist-dimension}
 \dim Q_p\le\dim H^1(K_p,\mathbb F_2)+2g.
\end{equation}
Kummer theory identifies $H^1(K_p,\mathbb F_2)$ with
$K_p^\times/K_p^{\times2}$. Let $R_p=\mathbb Z_{(p)}^h$.
For $u\in R_p^\times$, Hensel's lemma gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-henselian-unit-square-criteria}
 u\in K_p^{\times2}\ \Longleftrightarrow\
 \begin{cases}
   \overline u\in\mathbb F_p^{\times2},&p\ne2,\\
   u\equiv1\pmod{8R_2},&p=2.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
For odd $p$, choose $u_p\in R_p^\times$ with
$\overline u_p\notin\mathbb F_p^{\times2}$.
Every $a\in K_p^\times$ has the form $p^nu$ with
$n\in\mathbb Z$ and $u\in R_p^\times$.
Equation~\eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-henselian-unit-square-criteria}
therefore gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-local-kummer-bases}
 \begin{array}{lll}
 K_p^\times/K_p^{\times2}=\langle[p],[u_p]\rangle,
   &\dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^1(K_p,\mathbb F_2)=2,&p\ne2,\\
 K_2^\times/K_2^{\times2}=\langle[2],[-1],[5]\rangle,
   &\dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^1(K_2,\mathbb F_2)=3.&
 \end{array}
\end{equation}
For $p=2$, independence of $[-1]$ and $[5]$ follows from
$-1\equiv7\pmod8$ and $5\equiv5\pmod8$. The class $[2]$
is independent of $[-1]$ and $[5]$ because $v_2(2)=1$, whereas
$v_2(-1)=v_2(5)=0$. For odd $p$, independence of
$[p]$ and $[u_p]$ follows from the valuation and the residue class,
respectively. Equations~\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-local-twist-dimension}
and \eqref{eq:obs-mechanical-local-kummer-bases} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-local-twist-bounds}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}Q_p\le2g+2\quad(p\ne2),\qquad
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}Q_2\le2g+3.
\end{equation}
Since $\mathcal D_\Sigma$ is a quotient of
$\bigoplus_{p\in\Sigma}Q_p$, we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-difference-space-bound}
 \dim\mathcal D_\Sigma\le\sum_{p\in\Sigma}\dim Q_p
       \le(2g+2)t+(2g+3).
\end{equation}
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- proposition-23: body_md -->
\label{prop:uniform-realizable-obstruction}
Let $\widetilde S\to S_0\to S_{-1}$ be an anchored diagram, let
$g=g((S_{-1})_{\mathbb Q})$, and let $t=|\Sigma|-1$.  Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-uniform-obstruction-bound}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal O(\widetilde S,S_{-1})
 \le(326g+326)t+\frac{7g+7}{2}
 \le\frac{5000g^2}{\log g}.
\end{equation}
In particular the implicit constant in
$\dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal O(\widetilde S,S_{-1})=O(g^2/\log g)$ is universal.
<!-- end proposition-23 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
Substituting \eqref{eq:obs-explicit-local-dimension-bounds} into
\eqref{eq:bad-only-obstruction-budget} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-summed-obstruction-bound}
 \begin{aligned}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal O(\widetilde S,S_{-1})
 &\le(324g+324)t+\frac{3g+1}{2}
                    +(2g+2)t+(2g+3)\\
 &=(326g+326)t+\frac{7g+7}{2}.
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
It remains to give a numerical bound for $t$.  Write
$\vartheta(x)=\sum_{p\le x}\log p$ and
$\pi(x)=|\{p:p\text{ prime},\ p\le x\}|$.  Every prime $n<p\le2n$ divides
$\binom{2n}{n}$, so
$\vartheta(2n)-\vartheta(n)\le2n\log2$.
For $k\ge1$, summing the inequalities with
$n=2^j$, $0\le j\le k-1$, gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-chebyshev-dyadic-sum}
 \vartheta(2^k)
 \le\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2^{j+1}\log2
 =2(2^k-1)\log2.
\end{equation}
For $x\ge2$, choose the integer $k\ge1$ with
$2^{k-1}<x\le2^k$. Then
$\vartheta(x)\le\vartheta(2^k)\le4(\log2)x$.
There are at most $\sqrt x$ primes at most $\sqrt x$, and
$\log p>\tfrac12\log x$ for every prime $\sqrt x<p\le x$.
Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-prime-count-bound}
 \pi(x)\le\sqrt x+\frac{2\vartheta(x)}{\log x}
           \le\sqrt x+\frac{8(\log2)x}{\log x}.
\end{equation}
For $g\ge15$, one has $2g+1\le(31/15)g$ and $\log g\le\sqrt g$.
Substituting $x=2g+1$ into \eqref{eq:obs-explicit-prime-count-bound}
therefore gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-explicit-omitted-prime-count}
 t\le\pi(2g+1)
 \le\left(\sqrt{31/15}+\frac{248}{15}\log2\right)
                   \frac{g}{\log g}
 <\frac{14g}{\log g}.
\end{equation}
Also $326g+326<350g$ and $(7g+7)/2\le4g$.  By
\eqref{eq:obs-explicit-omitted-prime-count}, equation \eqref{eq:obs-explicit-summed-obstruction-bound} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:obs-mechanical-final-numerical-obstruction-bound}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal O(\widetilde S,S_{-1})
 <\frac{4900g^2}{\log g}+4g
 \le\frac{4904g^2}{\log g}
 <\frac{5000g^2}{\log g},
\end{equation}
as desired.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- proposition-24: body_md -->
\label{prop:constructed-tower}
Let $m\ge32768$, put $g=2^m-1$, and let $S_0\to S_{-1}$ and
$s_0:\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z\to S_0$ be the surfaces and section constructed in
Propositions~\ref{prop:start-integral-galois} and~\ref{prop:start-integral-section}.
There is a tower
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-surface-tower}
 \cdots\longrightarrow S_{n+1}\longrightarrow S_n
       \longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow S_0
\end{equation}
with the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item Every $S_n$ is an integral regular projective flat arithmetic
surface with smooth geometrically integral generic fibre.
\item The map $S_n\to S_0$ is a connected finite \'etale Galois
cover of degree $2^n$.  Each transition $S_{n+1}\to S_n$ has degree
two.
\item The composite $S_n\to S_{-1}$ is Galois. Put
$G_n=\operatorname{Gal}(S_n/S_{-1})$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-group-invariants}
 G_0\simeq\mathbb F_2^g,\qquad |G_n|=2^{g+n},\qquad d(G_n)=g.
\end{equation}
For every $n\ge0$, restriction gives a nonsplit central extension
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-central-extension}
 1\longrightarrow\mathbb F_2\longrightarrow G_{n+1}
       \longrightarrow G_n\longrightarrow1.
\end{equation}
\item The section $s_0$ splits completely at every level:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-section-splitting}
 S_n\times_{S_0,s_0}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z
       \simeq\coprod_{j=1}^{2^n}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z.
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-24 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Since $m\ge32768$ and $g=2^m-1\ge2^{m-1}$, we have
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-numerical-threshold}
 \log g\ge(m-1)\log2\ge32767\log2>20000,
 \qquad
 \frac{5000g^2}{\log g}<\frac{g^2}{4}.
\end{equation}
For $n=0$, we have $G_0=\operatorname{Gal}(S_0/S_{-1})\simeq\mathbb F_2^g$, by
Proposition~\ref{prop:start-integral-galois}.  Suppose $S_n$ has
been constructed, with $S_n\to S_0$ connected finite \'etale and
$S_n\to S_{-1}$ Galois with a finite $2$-group $G_n$ satisfying
$d(G_n)=g$.  By Proposition~\ref{prop:starting-anchor}, the diagram
$S_n\to S_0\to S_{-1}$ satisfies the conditions in
\eqref{eq:anchored-diagram}. The obstruction estimate of
Proposition~\ref{prop:uniform-realizable-obstruction}, together with
\eqref{eq:final-explicit-numerical-threshold} and \eqref{eq:finite-GS}, gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-positive-realizable-dimension}
 \dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathcal O(S_n,S_{-1})
 \le\frac{5000g^2}{\log g}
 <\frac{g^2}{4}
 <\dim_{\mathbb F_2}H^2(G_n,\mathbb F_2).
\end{equation}
Since
$\mathcal O(S_n,S_{-1})=H^2(G_n,\mathbb F_2)/\mathscr E(S_n,S_{-1})$,
\eqref{eq:final-explicit-positive-realizable-dimension} shows that
$\mathscr E(S_n,S_{-1})$ contains a nonzero class $\theta_n$.

Choose a realization $S_{n+1}\to S_n$ of $\theta_n$.
By the definition of realizability, $S_{n+1}\to S_n$ is connected and finite \'etale,
$S_{n+1}\to S_{-1}$ is Galois, and
$G_{n+1}=\operatorname{Gal}(S_{n+1}/S_{-1})$ embeds in $E_{\theta_n}$
so that restriction to $S_n$ is the composite
$G_{n+1}\hookrightarrow E_{\theta_n}\to G_n$.
The homomorphism $G_{n+1}\to G_n$ is surjective. Since
$\theta_n\ne0$, the extension $E_{\theta_n}\to G_n$ is nonsplit.
Apply \eqref{eq:final-subgroup-surjection} with
$E=E_{\theta_n}$ and $J=G_{n+1}$ to obtain $G_{n+1}=E_{\theta_n}$.
This proves \eqref{eq:final-explicit-central-extension}. In particular,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-induction-degree}
 |\ker(G_{n+1}\to G_n)|=2,\qquad
 [\mathbb Q(S_{n+1}):\mathbb Q(S_n)]=2,\qquad
 |G_{n+1}|=2|G_n|.
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:final-generator-equality} gives
$d(G_{n+1})=d(G_n)=g$. This completes the induction and proves
\eqref{eq:final-explicit-group-invariants}.

Each composite $S_n\to S_0$ is finite \'etale of degree $2^n$.
The field extension $\mathbb Q(S_n)/\mathbb Q(S_0)$ is Galois because
$\mathbb Q(S_n)/\mathbb Q(S_{-1})$ is Galois and
$\mathbb Q(S_{-1})\subseteq\mathbb Q(S_0)\subseteq\mathbb Q(S_n)$. Moreover,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-galois-kernel}
 \operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb Q(S_n)/\mathbb Q(S_0))=\ker(G_n\to G_0).
\end{equation}
The finite \'etale map $S_n\to S_0$ makes $S_n$ regular and hence
normal, so $S_n$ is the normalization of $S_0$ in $\mathbb Q(S_n)$.
Every automorphism of $\mathbb Q(S_n)$ fixing
$\mathbb Q(S_0)$ therefore extends to an automorphism of $S_n$ over
$S_0$, proving that $S_n\to S_0$ is Galois. Finally,
Proposition~\ref{prop:realizing-surface-properties}, applied to
$S_n\to S_0$, proves (i) and the splitting formula
\eqref{eq:final-explicit-section-splitting} in (iv).
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
Take $S=S_0$ and $s=s_0$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:constructed-tower}.
Assertions (i), (ii) and (iv) of Proposition~\ref{prop:constructed-tower}
are assertions (ii), (i) and (iii), respectively, of
Theorem~\ref{thm:main-surface}.
The connected Galois covers $S_n\to S$ give quotients
of $\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(S)$ of orders $2^n$, so $\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(S)$ is infinite.

Put $H=\operatorname{im}(\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(S_{\overline\mathbb Q})\to\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(S))$.
Let $Q$ be a finite quotient of $\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(S)$, and let $T\to S$
be the corresponding connected finite \'etale Galois cover with
group $Q$. By Proposition~\ref{prop:realizing-surface-properties},
$T_{\overline\mathbb Q}$ is connected. Hence the image of
$\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(S_{\overline\mathbb Q})$ in $Q$ acts transitively on the fibre
of $T\to S$ over a geometric point of $S_{\overline\mathbb Q}$.
Choose a geometric point $\bar x$ of $S_{\overline\mathbb Q}$ and identify
the fibre $T_{\bar x}$ with $Q$ by choosing one point of $T_{\bar x}$.
The image of $H$ in $Q$ then acts on $Q$ by left multiplication.
Transitivity implies $\operatorname{im}(H\to Q)=Q$. Since $Q$ was arbitrary,
$\overline H=\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(S)$. The subgroup $H$ is compact, as the
continuous image of the profinite group $\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(S_{\overline\mathbb Q})$,
and is therefore closed in $\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(S)$. Hence
$\pi_1^{\mathrm{geom}}(S)=H=\overline H=\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(S)$.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
Fix $m\ge32768$, put $b=2^m-1$, and take the tower of
Proposition~\ref{prop:constructed-tower}. For each $n\ge0$, put
$C_n=(S_n)_{\mathbb Q}$. Then $C_n$ is a smooth projective geometrically
integral curve over $\mathbb Q$.
The maps $C_n\to C_{-1}$ are Galois with groups $G_n$ of order
$2^{b+n}$ by \eqref{eq:final-explicit-group-invariants}.
The maps $C_n\to C_0$ are finite \'etale of degree
$2^n$, by base change from $S_n\to S_0$ to $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q$.
The map $C_0\to C_{-1}$ is \'etale by
Proposition~\ref{prop:start-etale}, so the composites
$C_n\to C_{-1}$ are \'etale. Each transition
$C_{n+1}\to C_n$ has degree two.
Applying Riemann--Hurwitz to $C_n\to C_{-1}$, whose target has
genus $b$, gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-generic-genus}
 g(C_n)-1=2^{b+n}(b-1).
\end{equation}

Let $s_{-1}:\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z\to S_{-1}$ be the section extending
$P_+\in C_{-1}(\mathbb Q)$, and put $A=\operatorname{Gal}(S_0/S_{-1})$. For $a\in A$,
put $s_a=a\circ s_0:\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z\to S_0$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:start-integral-section} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-base-translated-sections}
 S_0\times_{S_{-1},s_{-1}}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z
 \simeq\coprod_{a\in A}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z,
\end{equation}
where the map from the copy indexed by $a$ to $S_0$ is $s_a$.
Apply Proposition~\ref{prop:realizing-surface-properties} to each $s_a$
and the degree-$2^n$ finite \'etale cover $S_n\to S_0$. For each
$a\in A$, the fibre product $S_n\times_{S_0,s_a}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z$ is
a disjoint union of $2^n$ copies of $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z$.
Taking the disjoint union over $a\in A$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-translated-section-splitting}
 S_n\times_{S_{-1},s_{-1}}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z
       \simeq\coprod_{j=1}^{2^{b+n}}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z.
\end{equation}
Base change of \eqref{eq:final-explicit-translated-section-splitting}
from $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z$ to $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q$ identifies
$C_n\times_{C_{-1},P_+}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q$ with a disjoint union of
$2^{b+n}$ copies of $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q$. Thus $P_+$ splits completely
under $C_n\to C_{-1}$ and $\#C_n(\mathbb Q)\ge2^{b+n}$.

Choose subgroups $H_0,\ldots,H_b$ of $A$ satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-subgroup-flag}
 A=H_0\supset H_1\supset\cdots\supset H_b=1,
 \qquad [A:H_i]=2^i,
\end{equation}
in $A=\operatorname{Gal}(C_0/C_{-1})\simeq\mathbb F_2^b$, and set $E_i=C_0/H_i$
for $0\le i\le b$. Each $H_i$ is normal in the abelian group
$A$, so $E_i\to C_{-1}$ is Galois with group $A/H_i$ and
degree $2^i$ by \eqref{eq:final-explicit-subgroup-flag}.
The morphism $E_i\to C_{-1}$ is \'etale because $E_i=C_0/H_i$ is an intermediate cover
of the finite \'etale Galois cover $C_0\to C_{-1}$.
Set $E_{b+n}=C_n$ for $n\ge0$; the two definitions agree at
$E_b=C_0$. The curves $E_i$ form a tower of finite \'etale Galois
covers $E_i\to C_{-1}$ of degree $2^i$, with
$E_0=C_{-1}$, with transitions of degree two. For $0\le i\le b$, take the quotient by $H_i$ of the
$A$-torsor $C_0\times_{C_{-1},P_+}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q\simeq A\times\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q$.
For $i\ge b$, use \eqref{eq:final-explicit-translated-section-splitting}.
We obtain, for every $i\ge0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-all-intermediate-splitting}
 E_i\times_{C_{-1},P_+}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q
 \simeq\coprod_{j=1}^{2^i}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q.
\end{equation}

For every $g\ge3b$, Proposition~\ref{prop:genus-spreading} gives a
ramified double cover $f:C\to C_{-1}$ with $g(C)=g$ and a rational
point $P\in C(\mathbb Q)$ above $P_+$. Set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-pullback}
 D_i=C\times_{C_{-1}}E_i,\qquad
 \pi_i:D_i\longrightarrow C,\qquad (c,e)\longmapsto c.
\end{equation}
For each $i\ge0$, the morphism $D_{i+1}\to D_i$ is
$\operatorname{id}_C\times(E_{i+1}\to E_i)$. Also
$D_0=C\times_{C_{-1}}C_{-1}=C$.

Put $\Gamma_i=\operatorname{Gal}(E_i/C_{-1})$. Define the action of $\Gamma_i$ on $D_i$ by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-pullback-action}
 \gamma(c,e)=(c,\gamma e)\qquad(\gamma\in\Gamma_i).
\end{equation}
Thus $\pi_i$ is the base change of the finite \'etale
$\Gamma_i$-torsor $E_i\to C_{-1}$ and has degree $2^i$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:genus-spreading} shows that $D_i$ is geometrically
connected: the ramified quadratic extension
$\overline\mathbb Q(C)/\overline\mathbb Q(C_{-1})$ is linearly disjoint from
$\overline\mathbb Q(E_i)/\overline\mathbb Q(C_{-1})$, because
$(E_i)_{\overline\mathbb Q}\to(C_{-1})_{\overline\mathbb Q}$ is \'etale.
Hence $D_i$ is a smooth projective geometrically connected curve over $\mathbb Q$.

Riemann--Hurwitz for $\pi_i$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-pullback-genus}
 g(D_i)-1=2^i(g-1).
\end{equation}
Since $f(P)=P_+$, the fibre of $\pi_i$ at $P$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-pullback-splitting}
 D_i\times_{C,P}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q
 \simeq E_i\times_{C_{-1},P_+}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q
 \simeq\coprod_{j=1}^{2^i}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q.
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:final-explicit-pullback-splitting} shows that
$\pi_i^{-1}(P)$ consists of $2^i$ distinct $\mathbb Q$-rational points; hence
$P$ splits completely under $\pi_i:D_i\to C$ and $\#D_i(\mathbb Q)\ge2^i$.
Because $P$ splits completely, the section
$s_P:\operatorname{Gal}(\overline\mathbb Q/\mathbb Q)\to\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(C,\bar P)$ has trivial
image in $\operatorname{Gal}(D_i/C)$. The quotient map
$\pi_1^{\mathrm{\acute et}}(C,\bar P)\to\operatorname{Gal}(D_i/C)$ therefore factors through
$\pi_1^{\mathbb Q\text{-}\mathrm{geom}}(C,P)$. Since $|\operatorname{Gal}(D_i/C)|=2^i$ for every $i\ge0$,
$\pi_1^{\mathbb Q\text{-}\mathrm{geom}}(C,P)$ is infinite. This proves the theorem,
for example with $g_0=3(2^{32768}-1)$.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
Choose $m\ge32768$, put $g=2^m-1$, and take the tower of Proposition~\ref{prop:constructed-tower}. Write $C_n=(S_n)_{\mathbb Q}$.
Every $S_n\to S_0$ is finite \'etale.  The starting surface $S_0$ is
smooth over $U=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Z[1/\prod_{p\le2g+1}p]$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:start-good-fibres}.
Proposition~\ref{prop:conductor-uniform-integral-covers}, applied with
$\Sigma=\{p\text{ prime}:p\le2g+1\}$, therefore gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-explicit-conductor-bound}
 \log\mathfrak N(C_n)\le A_m g(C_n)\qquad(n\ge0),
 \qquad A_m=2\sum_{p\in\Sigma}(1+b_p)\log p,
\end{equation}
where, for each $p\in\Sigma$, we fix a finite Galois extension
$E_p/\mathbb Q_p$ over which $C_0$ has semistable reduction and put
$b_p=b(E_p/\mathbb Q_p)$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:conductor-local-bound}.
The extensions $E_p/\mathbb Q_p$ are chosen using $C_0$ and are independent
of $n$. Thus $A_m=2\sum_{p\in\Sigma}(1+b_p)\log p$ is independent of $n$.
The Riemann--Hurwitz formula \eqref{eq:final-explicit-generic-genus},
with $b=g$, gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-conductor-genus-limit}
 g(C_n)=1+2^{g+n}(g-1)\longrightarrow\infty
 \quad\text{as }n\to\infty.
\end{equation}
Base change of \eqref{eq:final-explicit-section-splitting}
to $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb Q$ shows that
$(s_0)_{\mathbb Q}\in C_0(\mathbb Q)$ splits completely under every map
$C_n\to C_0$.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
